EZEKIEL — LATIN–ENGLISH (CLEMENTINE VULGATE)
Captured from the Saints.Bible staging Parallel Reader, 1 November 2026.
Editorial revisions dated 9 November 2026. The capture date above identifies the source snapshot.
Verse numbers follow the Saints (Greek/Hebrew) column; 'vulgate_reference' in verses.json gives the Vulgate's own number where it differs.

EZEKIEL 1

1:1  LA: Et factum est in trigesimo anno, in quarto, in quinta mensis, cum essem in medio captivorum juxta fluvium Chobar, aperti sunt cæli, et vidi visiones Dei.
     EN: In the thirtieth year, in the fourth month, on the fifth day of the month, while I was among the captives beside the river Chebar, the heavens opened, and I saw visions of God.

1:2  LA: In quinta mensis, ipse est annus quintus transmigrationis regis Joachin,
     EN: On the fifth day of the month—it was the fifth year of King Jehoiachin’s exile—

1:3  LA: factum est verbum Domini ad Ezechielem filium Buzi sacerdotem, in terra Chaldæorum, secus flumen Chobar : et facta est super eum ibi manus Domini.
     EN: the word of the Lord came to Ezekiel the priest, the son of Buzi, in the land of the Chaldeans, beside the river Chebar; and the hand of the Lord came upon him there.

1:4  LA: Et vidi, et ecce ventus turbinis veniebat ab aquilone, et nubes magna, et ignis involvens, et splendor in circuitu ejus : et de medio ejus, quasi species electri, id est, de medio ignis :
     EN: I looked, and behold, a whirlwind was coming from the north, with a great cloud and enveloping fire and brightness around it. From its midst came something like the appearance of electrum—that is, from the midst of the fire.

1:5  LA: et in medio ejus similitudo quatuor animalium. Et hic aspectus eorum, similitudo hominis in eis.
     EN: Within it was the likeness of four living creatures. This was their appearance: they had a human likeness.

1:6  LA: Quatuor facies uni, et quatuor pennæ uni.
     EN: Each had four faces, and each had four wings.

1:7  LA: Pedes eorum, pedes recti, et planta pedis eorum quasi planta pedis vituli : et scintillæ quasi aspectus æris candentis.
     EN: Their legs were straight legs, and the sole of each foot was like the sole of a calf’s foot, with sparks like the appearance of glowing bronze.

1:8  LA: Et manus hominis sub pennis eorum, in quatuor partibus : et facies et pennas per quatuor partes habebant.
     EN: Human hands were under their wings on their four sides; and they had faces and wings on all four sides.

1:9  LA: Junctæque erant pennæ eorum alterius ad alterum : non revertebantur cum incederent, sed unumquodque ante faciem suam gradiebatur.
     EN: Their wings were joined to one another. They did not turn back as they went, but each advanced straight ahead.

1:10  LA: Similitudo autem vultus eorum, facies hominis et facies leonis a dextris ipsorum quatuor, facies autem bovis a sinistris ipsorum quatuor, et facies aquilæ desuper ipsorum quatuor.
      EN: As for the likeness of their faces, each of the four had a human face, and a lion’s face on the right; each of the four had an ox’s face on the left, and an eagle’s face above.

1:11  LA: Facies eorum et pennæ eorum extentæ desuper : duæ pennæ singulorum jungebantur, et duæ tegebant corpora eorum.
      EN: Their faces and their wings were stretched out above; two wings of each were joined together, and two covered their bodies.

1:12  LA: Et unumquodque eorum coram facie sua ambulabat : ubi erat impetus spiritus, illuc gradiebantur, nec revertebantur cum ambularent.
      EN: Each went straight ahead. Where the impulse of the spirit was, there they advanced, and they did not turn back as they went.

1:13  LA: Et similitudo animalium, aspectus eorum quasi carbonum ignis ardentium, et quasi aspectus lampadarum : hæc erat visio discurrens in medio animalium, splendor ignis, et de igne fulgur egrediens.
      EN: The likeness of the creatures, their appearance, was like burning coals of fire and like the appearance of torches. This was the sight moving to and fro among the creatures: the brightness of fire, and lightning coming out of the fire.

1:15  LA: Cumque aspicerem animalia, apparuit rota una super terram juxta animalia, habens quatuor facies.
      EN: As I looked at the creatures, one wheel appeared on the ground beside the creatures, having four faces.

1:16  LA: Et aspectus rotarum et opus earum quasi visio maris : et una similitudo ipsarum quatuor : et aspectus earum et opera quasi sit rota in medio rotæ.
      EN: The appearance of the wheels and their workmanship was like the appearance of the sea, and all four had one likeness. Their appearance and workmanship were as though a wheel were within a wheel.

1:17  LA: Per quatuor partes earum euntes ibant, et non revertebantur cum ambularent.
      EN: They went in their four directions as they moved, and did not turn back as they went.

1:18  LA: Statura quoque erat rotis, et altitudo, et horribilis aspectus : et totum corpus oculis plenum in circuitu ipsarum quatuor.
      EN: The wheels also had stature and height and a terrifying appearance; and the whole body of all four was full of eyes all around.

1:19  LA: Cumque ambularent animalia, ambulabant pariter et rotæ juxta ea : et cum elevarentur animalia de terra, elevabantur simul et rotæ.
      EN: When the creatures moved, the wheels moved together beside them; and when the creatures rose from the ground, the wheels rose with them.

1:20  LA: Quocumque ibat spiritus, illuc, eunte spiritu, et rotæ pariter elevabantur sequentes eum : spiritus enim vitæ erat in rotis.
      EN: Wherever the spirit went, there, as the spirit went, the wheels also rose together, following it, for the spirit of life was in the wheels.

1:21  LA: Cum euntibus ibant, et cum stantibus stabant : et cum elevatis a terra, pariter elevabantur et rotæ sequentes ea, quia spiritus vitæ erat in rotis.
      EN: With the creatures moving, they moved; with them standing, they stood; and with them rising from the ground, the wheels also rose together, following them, because the spirit of life was in the wheels.

1:22  LA: Et similitudo super capita animalium firmamenti, quasi aspectus crystalli horribilis, et extenti super capita eorum desuper.
      EN: Above the creatures’ heads was the likeness of a firmament, like the appearance of awe-inspiring crystal, stretched out above their heads.

1:23  LA: Sub firmamento autem pennæ eorum rectæ alterius ad alterum : unumquodque duabus alis velabat corpus suum, et alterum similiter velabatur.
      EN: Under the firmament their wings were straight, one toward another. Each covered its body with two wings, and the next was covered in the same way.

1:24  LA: Et audiebam sonum alarum, quasi sonum aquarum multarum, quasi sonum sublimis Dei : cum ambularent, quasi sonus erat multitudinis ut sonus castrorum : cumque starent, demittebantur pennæ eorum.
      EN: I heard the sound of the wings, like the sound of many waters, like the sound of God on high. When they moved, it was like the sound of a multitude, like the sound of an army’s camp; and when they stood still, their wings were lowered.

1:25  LA: Nam cum fieret vox super firmamentum quod erat super caput eorum, stabant, et submittebant alas suas.
      EN: For when a voice came from above the firmament over their heads, they stood still and lowered their wings.

1:26  LA: Et super firmamentum quod erat imminens capiti eorum, quasi aspectus lapidis sapphiri similitudo throni : et super similitudinem throni similitudo quasi aspectus hominis desuper.
      EN: Above the firmament that hung over their heads was the likeness of a throne, like the appearance of sapphire; and upon the likeness of the throne, above it, was a likeness like the appearance of a man.

1:27  LA: Et vidi quasi speciem electri, velut aspectum ignis, intrinsecus ejus per circuitum : a lumbis ejus et desuper, et a lumbis ejus usque deorsum, vidi quasi speciem ignis splendentis in circuitu,
      EN: I saw something like the appearance of electrum, like the appearance of fire within it all around. From His waist upward, and from His waist downward, I saw something like the appearance of fire shining all around,

1:28  LA: velut aspectum arcus cum fuerit in nube in die pluviæ. Hic erat aspectus splendoris per gyrum.
      EN: like the appearance of a rainbow when it is in a cloud on a rainy day. This was the appearance of the brightness all around.

EZEKIEL 2

2:1  LA: 
     EN: 

2:2  LA: Et ingressus est in me spiritus postquam locutus est mihi, et statuit me supra pedes meos : et audivi loquentem ad me,
     EN: A spirit entered me after He had spoken to me and set me on my feet; and I heard Him speaking to me

2:3  LA: et dicentem : Fili hominis, mitto ego te ad filios Israël, ad gentes apostatrices quæ recesserunt a me : ipsi et patres eorum prævaricati sunt pactum meum usque ad diem hanc :
     EN: and saying, “Son of man, I am sending you to the children of Israel, to apostate nations that have withdrawn from Me. They and their fathers have violated My covenant to this very day.

2:4  LA: et filii dura facie et indomabili corde sunt, ad quos ego mitto te. Et dices ad eos : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus :
     EN: Their children are hard-faced and untamable in heart; to them I am sending you. You shall say to them, ‘Thus says the Lord God,’

2:5  LA: si forte vel ipsi audiant, et si forte quiescant, quoniam domus exasperans est : et scient quia propheta fuerit in medio eorum.
     EN: in case even they may listen, and in case they may desist, for they are an exasperating house. They will know that a prophet has been among them.

2:6  LA: Tu ergo, fili hominis, ne timeas eos, neque sermones eorum metuas, quoniam increduli et subversores sunt tecum, et cum scorpionibus habitas. Verba eorum ne timeas, et vultus eorum ne formides, quia domus exasperans est.
     EN: You, therefore, son of man, do not fear them or be afraid of their words, for unbelieving men and subverters are with you, and you dwell with scorpions. Do not fear their words or be terrified at their faces, for they are an exasperating house.

2:7  LA: Loqueris ergo verba mea ad eos, si forte audiant, et quiescant : quoniam irritatores sunt.
     EN: You shall speak My words to them, in case they may listen and desist, for they are provokers.

2:8  LA: Tu autem, fili hominis, audi quæcumque loquor ad te, et noli esse exasperans, sicut domus exasperatrix est : aperi os tuum, et comede quæcumque ego do tibi.
     EN: But you, son of man, hear whatever I speak to you, and do not be exasperating like this exasperating house. Open your mouth and eat whatever I give you.”

2:9  LA: Et vidi : et ecce manus missa ad me, in qua erat involutus liber : et expandit illum coram me, qui erat scriptus intus et foris : et scriptæ erant in eo lamentationes, et carmen, et væ.
     EN: I looked, and behold, a hand was stretched out toward me, and in it was a rolled-up book. He unrolled it before me; it was written on inside and outside, and written in it were lamentations, a song, and woe.

2:10  LA: 
      EN: 

EZEKIEL 3

3:1  LA: Et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, quodcumque inveneris, comede : comede volumen istud, et vadens loquere ad filios Israël.
     EN: He said to me, “Son of man, eat whatever you find. Eat this scroll, and go and speak to the children of Israel.”

3:2  LA: Et aperui os meum, et cibavit me volumine illo :
     EN: I opened my mouth, and He fed me that scroll.

3:3  LA: et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, venter tuus comedet, et viscera tua complebuntur volumine isto quod ego do tibi. Et comedi illud, et factum est in ore meo sicut mel dulce.
     EN: He said to me, “Son of man, your stomach shall eat, and your inward parts shall be filled with this scroll that I am giving you.” I ate it, and in my mouth it became as sweet as honey.

3:4  LA: Et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, vade ad domum Israël, et loqueris verba mea ad eos.
     EN: He said to me, “Son of man, go to the house of Israel and speak My words to them.

3:5  LA: Non enim ad populum profundi sermonis et ignotæ linguæ tu mitteris ad domum Israël :
     EN: For you are not being sent to a people of obscure speech and an unknown language, but to the house of Israel;

3:6  LA: neque ad populos multos profundi sermonis et ignotæ linguæ, quorum non possis audire sermones : et si ad illos mittereris, ipsi audirent te :
     EN: nor to many peoples of obscure speech and an unknown language, whose words you cannot understand. If you were sent to them, they would listen to you.

3:7  LA: domus autem Israël nolunt audire te, quia nolunt audire me : omnis quippe domus Israël attrita fronte est, et duro corde.
     EN: But the house of Israel will not listen to you, because they will not listen to Me. The whole house of Israel is indeed brazen-faced and hard-hearted.

3:8  LA: Ecce dedi faciem tuam valentiorem faciebus eorum, et frontem tuam duriorem frontibus eorum :
     EN: Behold, I have made your face stronger than their faces and your forehead harder than their foreheads.

3:9  LA: ut adamantem et ut silicem dedi faciem tuam : ne timeas eos, neque metuas a facie eorum, quia domus exasperans est.
     EN: I have made your face like adamant and like flint. Do not fear them or be afraid before them, for they are an exasperating house.”

3:10  LA: Et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, omnes sermones meos quos ego loquor ad te, assume in corde tuo, et auribus tuis audi :
      EN: He said to me, “Son of man, take into your heart all My words that I speak to you, and hear them with your ears.

3:11  LA: et vade, ingredere ad transmigrationem, ad filios populi tui, et loqueris ad eos : et dices eis : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : si forte audiant et quiescant.
      EN: Go, enter among the exiles, the children of your people, and speak to them. Say to them, ‘Thus says the Lord God,’ in case they may listen and desist.”

3:12  LA: Et assumpsit me spiritus, et audivi post me vocem commotionis magnæ : Benedicta gloria Domini de loco suo :
      EN: A spirit took me up, and I heard behind me the sound of a great shaking: “Blessed be the glory of the Lord from His place!”

3:13  LA: et vocem alarum animalium percutientium alteram ad alteram, et vocem rotarum sequentium animalia, et vocem commotionis magnæ.
      EN: I heard the sound of the creatures’ wings striking one another, the sound of the wheels following the creatures, and the sound of a great shaking.

3:14  LA: Spiritus quoque levavit me, et assumpsit me : et abii amarus in indignatione spiritus mei : manus enim Domini erat mecum, confortans me.
      EN: The spirit lifted me and took me up, and I went in bitterness, in the indignation of my spirit, for the hand of the Lord was with me, strengthening me.

3:15  LA: Et veni ad transmigrationem, ad acervum novarum frugum, ad eos qui habitabant juxta flumen Chobar : et sedi ubi illi sedebant, et mansi ibi septem diebus mœrens in medio eorum.
      EN: I came to the exiles, to the heap of new crops, to those who lived beside the river Chebar. I sat where they sat, and remained there for seven days, mourning among them.

3:16  LA: Cum autem pertransissent septem dies, factum est verbum Domini ad me, dicens :
      EN: When seven days had passed, the word of the Lord came to me, saying,

3:17  LA: Fili hominis, speculatorem dedi te domui Israël, et audies de ore meo verbum, et annuntiabis eis ex me.
      EN: “Son of man, I have appointed you a watchman for the house of Israel. You shall hear a word from My mouth and give them warning from Me.

3:18  LA: Si, dicente me ad impium : Morte morieris, non annuntiaveris ei, neque locutus fueris ut avertatur a via sua impia et vivat, ipse impius in iniquitate sua morietur, sanguinem autem ejus de manu tua requiram.
      EN: If, when I say to the wicked man, ‘You will surely die,’ you do not warn him or speak so that he may turn from his wicked way and live, that wicked man will die in his iniquity, but I will require his blood at your hand.

3:19  LA: Si autem tu annuntiaveris impio, et ille non fuerit conversus ab impietate sua et a via sua impia, ipse quidem in iniquitate sua morietur : tu autem animam tuam liberasti.
      EN: But if you warn the wicked man and he does not turn from his wickedness and his wicked way, he will indeed die in his iniquity, but you have delivered your own soul.

3:20  LA: Sed et si conversus justus a justitia sua fuerit, et fecerit iniquitatem, ponam offendiculum coram eo : ipse morietur quia non annuntiasti ei. In peccato suo morietur, et non erunt in memoria justitiæ ejus quas fecit : sanguinem vero ejus de manu tua requiram.
      EN: Again, if a righteous man turns from his righteousness and does iniquity, I will place a stumbling block before him. He will die because you did not warn him. He will die in his sin, and the righteous deeds he has done will not be remembered; but I will require his blood at your hand.

3:21  LA: Si autem tu annuntiaveris justo ut non peccet justus, et ille non peccaverit, vivens vivet, quia annuntiasti ei, et tu animam tuam liberasti.
      EN: But if you warn the righteous man not to sin, and he does not sin, he will surely live because you warned him, and you have delivered your own soul.”

3:22  LA: Et facta est super me manus Domini, et dixit ad me : Surgens egredere in campum, et ibi loquar tecum.
      EN: The hand of the Lord came upon me, and He said to me, “Rise, go out to the plain, and there I will speak with you.”

3:23  LA: Et surgens egressus sum in campum : et ecce ibi gloria Domini stabat, quasi gloria quam vidi juxta fluvium Chobar : et cecidi in faciem meam.
      EN: I rose and went out to the plain; and behold, the glory of the Lord was standing there, like the glory I had seen beside the river Chebar, and I fell on my face.

3:24  LA: Et ingressus est in me spiritus, et statuit me super pedes meos, et locutus est mihi, et dixit ad me : Ingredere, et includere in medio domus tuæ.
      EN: A spirit entered me and set me on my feet. He spoke to me and said, “Go in and shut yourself inside your house.

3:25  LA: Et tu, fili hominis, ecce data sunt super te vincula, et ligabunt te in eis, et non egredieris de medio eorum.
      EN: As for you, son of man, look, they will put bonds on you and bind you with them, and you will not go out from among them.

3:26  LA: Et linguam tuam adhærere faciam palato tuo, et eris mutus, nec quasi vir objurgans, quia domus exasperans est.
      EN: I will make your tongue cling to the roof of your mouth, and you will be mute and unable to act as a man who reproves, because they are an exasperating house.

3:27  LA: Cum autem locutus fuero tibi, aperiam os tuum, et dices ad eos : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Qui audit, audiat, et qui quiescit, quiescat : quia domus exasperans est.
      EN: But when I speak to you, I will open your mouth, and you shall say to them, ‘Thus says the Lord God.’ Let whoever hears, hear, and whoever desists, desist, for they are an exasperating house.

EZEKIEL 4

4:1  LA: Et tu, fili hominis, sume tibi laterem, et pones eum coram te, et describes in eo civitatem Jerusalem.
     EN: “And you, son of man, take a brick, set it before you, and draw the city of Jerusalem on it.

4:2  LA: Et ordinabis adversus eam obsidionem, et ædificabis munitiones, et comportabis aggerem, et dabis contra eam castra, et pones arietes in gyro.
     EN: Lay siege to it: build siege works, heap up a ramp, set camps against it, and place battering rams all around.

4:3  LA: Et tu sume tibi sartaginem ferream, et pones eam in murum ferreum inter te et inter civitatem : et obfirmabis faciem tuam ad eam, et erit in obsidionem, et circumdabis eam : signum est domui Israël.
     EN: Take an iron pan, and set it as an iron wall between yourself and the city. Set your face firmly against it, and it will be under siege, and you shall surround it. This is a sign for the house of Israel.

4:4  LA: Et tu dormies super latus tuum sinistrum, et pones iniquitates domus Israël super eo, numero dierum quibus dormies super illud : et assumes iniquitatem eorum.
     EN: Lie on your left side, and place the iniquities of the house of Israel upon it. For the number of days you lie on it, you shall bear their iniquity.

4:5  LA: Ego autem dedi tibi annos iniquitatis eorum, numero dierum trecentos et nonaginta dies : et portabis iniquitatem domus Israël.
     EN: I have assigned you the years of their iniquity as a number of days: three hundred and ninety days. You shall bear the iniquity of the house of Israel.

4:6  LA: Et cum compleveris hæc, dormies super latus tuum dexterum secundo, et assumes iniquitatem domus Juda quadraginta diebus : diem pro anno, diem, inquam, pro anno, dedi tibi.
     EN: When you have completed these, lie on your right side a second time, and bear the iniquity of the house of Judah for forty days. I have assigned you a day for a year—a day, I say, for a year.

4:7  LA: Et ad obsidionem Jerusalem convertes faciem tuam, et brachium tuum erit extentum : et prophetabis adversus eam.
     EN: Turn your face toward the siege of Jerusalem, and your arm shall be stretched out; and you shall prophesy against it.

4:8  LA: Ecce circumdedi te vinculis : et non te convertes a latere tuo in latus aliud, donec compleas dies obsidionis tuæ.
     EN: Behold, I have surrounded you with bonds, and you shall not turn from one side to the other until you complete the days of your siege.

4:9  LA: Et tu, sume tibi frumentum, et hordeum, et fabam, et lentem, et milium, et viciam : et mittes ea in vas unum, et facies tibi panes numero dierum quibus dormies super latus tuum : trecentis et nonaginta diebus comedes illud.
     EN: Take wheat, barley, beans, lentils, millet, and vetch. Put them into one vessel and make bread for yourself for the number of days you lie on your side; for three hundred and ninety days you shall eat it.

4:10  LA: Cibus autem tuus, quo vesceris, erit in pondere viginti stateres in die : a tempore usque ad tempus comedes illud.
      EN: The food you eat shall weigh twenty staters a day; you shall eat it at set intervals.

4:11  LA: Et aquam in mensura bibes, sextam partem hin : a tempore usque ad tempus bibes illud.
      EN: You shall drink water by measure, a sixth of a hin; you shall drink it at set intervals.

4:12  LA: Et quasi subcinericium hordeaceum comedes illud, et stercore quod egreditur de homine operies illud in oculis eorum.
      EN: You shall eat it like a barley cake baked under ashes, and cover it with the dung that comes out of a man, before their eyes.”

4:13  LA: Et dixit Dominus : Sic comedent filii Israël panem suum pollutum inter gentes ad quas ejiciam eos. Et dixi :
      EN: The Lord said, “Thus the children of Israel will eat their bread defiled among the nations to which I will drive them out.” And I said,

4:14  LA: A, a, a, Domine Deus, ecce anima mea non est polluta : et morticinum, et laceratum a bestiis non comedi ab infantia mea usque nunc, et non est ingressa in os meum omnis caro immunda.
      EN: “Ah, ah, ah, Lord God! Behold, my soul has not been defiled. From my infancy until now I have eaten neither what died of itself nor what was torn by beasts, and no unclean meat has entered my mouth.”

4:15  LA: Et dixit ad me : Ecce dedi tibi fimum boum pro stercoribus humanis, et facies panem tuum in eo.
      EN: He said to me, “Behold, I have given you cattle dung in place of human dung, and you shall make your bread over it.”

4:16  LA: Et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, ecce ego conteram baculum panis in Jerusalem, et comedent panem in pondere et in sollicitudine, et aquam in mensura et in angustia bibent,
      EN: He said to me, “Son of man, behold, I will break the staff of bread in Jerusalem. They will eat bread by weight and with anxiety, and drink water by measure and in distress,

4:17  LA: ut deficientibus pane et aqua, corruat unusquisque ad fratrem suum, et contabescant in iniquitatibus suis.
      EN: so that, when bread and water fail, each may collapse beside his brother, and they may waste away in their iniquities.

EZEKIEL 5

5:1  LA: Et tu, fili hominis, sume tibi gladium acutum, radentem pilos, et assumes eum et duces per caput tuum et per barbam tuam, et assumes tibi stateram ponderis et divides eos.
     EN: “And you, son of man, take a sharp sword that shaves off hair. Take it and pass it over your head and your beard; then take a weighing scale and divide the hair.

5:2  LA: Tertiam partem igni combures in medio civitatis, juxta completionem dierum obsidionis, et assumes tertiam partem, et concides gladio in circuitu ejus : tertiam vero aliam disperges in ventum, et gladium nudabo post eos.
     EN: You shall burn a third part with fire in the middle of the city when the days of the siege are completed. Take a third part and cut it with the sword all around the city; and scatter the remaining third part to the wind, and I will draw a sword after them.

5:3  LA: Et sumes inde parvum numerum, et ligabis eos in summitate pallii tui :
     EN: Take a small number of hairs from there and bind them in the edge of your cloak.

5:4  LA: et ex eis rursum tolles, et projicies eos in medio ignis, et combures eos igni, et ex eo egredietur ignis in omnem domum Israël.
     EN: Take some of these again, throw them into the midst of the fire, and burn them with fire; and from it fire will go out into the whole house of Israel.

5:5  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Ista est Jerusalem : in medio gentium posui eam, et in circuitu ejus terras.
     EN: “Thus says the Lord God: This is Jerusalem. I placed her in the midst of the nations, with lands all around her.

5:6  LA: Et contempsit judicia mea, ut plus esset impia quam gentes, et præcepta mea ultra quam terræ quæ in circuitu ejus sunt : judicia enim mea projecerunt, et in præceptis meis non ambulaverunt.
     EN: She has despised My judgments, becoming more wicked than the nations, and My commands, beyond the lands around her. For they have cast aside My judgments and have not walked in My commands.

5:7  LA: Idcirco hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Quia superastis gentes quæ in circuitu vestro sunt, et in præceptis meis non ambulastis, et judicia mea non fecistis, et juxta judicia gentium quæ in circuitu vestro sunt non estis operati,
     EN: Therefore thus says the Lord God: Because you have surpassed the nations around you, and have not walked in My commands or carried out My judgments, and have not even acted according to the judgments of the nations around you,

5:8  LA: ideo hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Ecce ego ad te, et ipse ego faciam in medio tui judicia in oculis gentium :
     EN: therefore thus says the Lord God: Behold, I am against you, and I Myself will execute judgments in your midst before the eyes of the nations.

5:9  LA: et faciam in te quod non feci, et quibus similia ultra non faciam, propter omnes abominationes tuas.
     EN: Because of all your abominations, I will do in you what I have never done, and the like of which I will never do again.

5:10  LA: Ideo patres comedent filios in medio tui, et filii comedent patres suos : et faciam in te judicia, et ventilabo universas reliquias tuas in omnem ventum.
      EN: Therefore fathers will eat their sons in your midst, and sons will eat their fathers. I will execute judgments in you and scatter all your survivors to every wind.

5:11  LA: Idcirco vivo ego, dicit Dominus Deus, nisi pro eo quod sanctum meum violasti in omnibus offensionibus tuis et in cunctis abominationibus tuis, ego quoque confringam : et non parcet oculus meus, et non miserebor.
      EN: Therefore, as I live, says the Lord God, surely, because you have violated My sanctuary with all your offenses and all your abominations, I too will shatter you. My eye will not spare, and I will not have mercy.

5:12  LA: Tertia pars tui peste morietur, et fame consumetur in medio tui, et tertia pars tui in gladio cadet in circuitu tuo : tertiam vero partem tuam in omnem ventum dispergam, et gladium evaginabo post eos.
      EN: A third part of you will die of pestilence and be consumed by famine in your midst; and a third part of you will fall by the sword all around you. A third part of you I will scatter to every wind, and I will unsheathe a sword after them.

5:13  LA: Et complebo furorem meum, et requiescere faciam indignationem meam in eis, et consolabor : et scient quia ego Dominus locutus sum in zelo meo, cum implevero indignationem meam in eis.
      EN: I will spend My fury, and cause My indignation to settle upon them, and I will be consoled. They will know that I, the Lord, have spoken in My zeal, when I have spent My indignation upon them.

5:14  LA: Et dabo te in desertum, et in opprobrium gentibus quæ in circuitu tuo sunt, in conspectu omnis prætereuntis :
      EN: I will make you a wasteland and a reproach among the nations around you, in the sight of everyone who passes by.

5:15  LA: et eris opprobrium et blasphemia, exemplum et stupor in gentibus quæ in circuitu tuo sunt, cum fecero in te judicia in furore, et in indignatione, et in increpationibus iræ.
      EN: You will be a reproach and an object of abuse, an example and an astonishment among the nations around you, when I execute judgments in you in fury, indignation, and angry rebukes.

5:16  LA: Ego Dominus locutus sum : quando misero sagittas famis pessimas in eos, quæ erunt mortiferæ, et quas mittam ut disperdam vos : et famem congregabo super vos, et conteram in vobis baculum panis :
      EN: I, the Lord, have spoken. When I send the deadly arrows of famine against them, arrows that bring death, which I will send to destroy you, I will heap famine upon you and break the staff of bread among you.

5:17  LA: et immittam in vos famem et bestias pessimas, usque ad internecionem : et pestilentia et sanguis transibunt per te, et gladium inducam super te. Ego Dominus locutus sum.
      EN: I will send famine and savage beasts upon you, even to extermination. Pestilence and bloodshed will pass through you, and I will bring a sword upon you. I, the Lord, have spoken.”

EZEKIEL 6

6:1  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini ad me, dicens :
     EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

6:2  LA: Fili hominis, pone faciem tuam ad montes Israël, et prophetabis ad eos,
     EN: “Son of man, set your face toward the mountains of Israel and prophesy to them.

6:3  LA: et dices : [Montes Israël, audite verbum Domini Dei. Hæc dicit Dominus Deus montibus et collibus, rupibus et vallibus : Ecce ego inducam super vos gladium, et disperdam excelsa vestra,
     EN: Say: Mountains of Israel, hear the word of the Lord God. Thus says the Lord God to the mountains and hills, to the rocks and valleys: Behold, I will bring a sword upon you and destroy your high places.

6:4  LA: et demoliar aras vestras, et confringentur simulacra vestra, et dejiciam interfectos vestros ante idola vestra :
     EN: I will demolish your altars, and your images will be broken. I will cast down your slain before your idols.

6:5  LA: et dabo cadavera filiorum Israël ante faciem simulacrorum vestrorum, et dispergam ossa vestra circum aras vestras :
     EN: I will lay the corpses of the children of Israel before your images and scatter your bones around your altars.

6:6  LA: in omnibus habitationibus vestris urbes desertæ erunt, et excelsa demolientur et dissipabuntur : et interibunt aræ vestræ, et confringentur, et cessabunt idola vestra, et conterentur delubra vestra, et delebuntur opera vestra :
     EN: In all your settlements the cities will be deserted, and the high places will be demolished and scattered. Your altars will perish and be broken; your idols will cease to exist, your shrines will be crushed, and your works will be wiped away.

6:7  LA: et cadet interfectus in medio vestri, et scietis quia ego sum Dominus.
     EN: The slain will fall in your midst, and you will know that I am the Lord.

6:8  LA: Et relinquam in vobis eos qui fugerint gladium in gentibus, cum dispersero vos in terris :
     EN: I will leave among you those who escape the sword among the nations, when I scatter you through the lands.

6:9  LA: et recordabuntur mei liberati vestri in gentibus ad quas captivi ducti sunt : quia contrivi cor eorum fornicans et recedens a me, et oculos eorum fornicantes post idola sua : et displicebunt sibimet super malis quæ fecerunt in universis abominationibus suis.
     EN: Your survivors will remember Me among the nations to which they have been led captive, because I have crushed their adulterous heart that turned away from Me, and their eyes that went whoring after their idols. They will be disgusted with themselves for the evils they have done in all their abominations.

6:10  LA: Et scient quia ego Dominus non frustra locutus sum, ut facerem eis malum hoc.
      EN: They will know that I, the Lord, did not speak in vain when I said I would bring this evil upon them.

6:11  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Percute manum tuam et allide pedem tuum, et dic : Heu ! ad omnes abominationes malorum domus Israël : quia gladio, fame et peste ruituri sunt.
      EN: Thus says the Lord God: Clap your hand and stamp your foot, and say, ‘Alas!’ over all the evil abominations of the house of Israel, for they will fall by sword, famine, and pestilence.

6:12  LA: Qui longe est, peste morietur : qui autem prope, gladio corruet : et qui relictus fuerit et obsessus, fame morietur : et complebo indignationem meam in eis.
      EN: Whoever is far away will die of pestilence; whoever is near will fall by the sword; and whoever is left and besieged will die of famine. I will spend My indignation upon them.

6:13  LA: Et scietis quia ego Dominus, cum fuerint interfecti vestri in medio idolorum vestrorum, in circuitu ararum vestrarum, in omni colle excelso, et in cunctis summitatibus montium, et subtus omne lignum nemorosum, et subtus universam quercum frondosam, locum ubi accenderunt thura redolentia universis idolis suis.
      EN: You will know that I am the Lord when your slain lie among your idols, around your altars, on every high hill and on all the mountaintops, beneath every shady tree and beneath every leafy oak, in the place where they burned fragrant incense to all their idols.

6:14  LA: Et extendam manum meam super eos : et faciam terram desolatam et destitutam, a deserto Deblatha, in omnibus habitationibus eorum : et scient quia ego Dominus.]
      EN: I will stretch out My hand against them and make the land desolate and abandoned, from the wilderness of Diblath, in all their settlements. They will know that I am the Lord.”

EZEKIEL 7

7:1  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini ad me, dicens :
     EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

7:2  LA: Et tu, fili hominis, hæc dicit Dominus Deus terræ Israël : [Finis venit : venit finis super quatuor plagas terræ.
     EN: “And you, son of man, thus says the Lord God to the land of Israel: The end has come! The end has come upon the four quarters of the land.

7:3  LA: Finis venit, venit finis : evigilavit adversum te, ecce venit.
     EN: The end has come, the end has come! It has awakened against you; behold, it comes.

7:4  LA: Venit contritio super te, qui habitas in terra : venit tempus, prope est dies occisionis, et non gloriæ montium.
     EN: Ruin has come upon you who dwell in the land. The time has come; the day of slaughter is near, and not of glory on the mountains.

7:5  LA: Nunc de propinquo effundam iram meam super te, et complebo furorem meum in te : et judicabo te juxta vias tuas, et imponam tibi omnia scelera tua,
     EN: Now, very soon, I will pour out My wrath upon you and spend My fury against you. I will judge you according to your ways and bring all your crimes upon you.

7:6  LA: et non parcet oculus meus, nec miserebor : sed vias tuas imponam tibi, et abominationes tuæ in medio tui erunt, et scietis quia ego sum Dominus percutiens.
     EN: My eye will not spare, and I will not have mercy. I will bring your ways upon you, and your abominations will be in your midst; and you will know that I am the Lord who strikes.

7:7  LA: Nunc finis super te, et immittam furorem meum in te : et judicabo te juxta vias tuas, et ponam contra te omnes abominationes tuas.
     EN: Now the end is upon you, and I will send My fury against you. I will judge you according to your ways, and set all your abominations against you.

7:8  LA: Et non parcet oculus meus super te, et non miserebor : sed vias tuas ponam super te, et abominationes tuæ in medio tui erunt, et scietis quia ego Dominus.
     EN: My eye will not spare you, and I will not have mercy. I will bring your ways upon you, and your abominations will be in your midst; and you will know that I am the Lord.

7:9  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Afflictio una, afflictio ecce venit.
     EN: Thus says the Lord God: One affliction—behold, an affliction is coming!

7:10  LA: Ecce dies, ecce venit : egressa est contritio, floruit virga, germinavit superbia,
      EN: Behold the day! Behold, it comes! Ruin has gone forth, the rod has blossomed, pride has sprouted.

7:11  LA: iniquitas surrexit in virga impietatis : non ex eis, et non ex populo, neque ex sonitu eorum : et non erit requies in eis.
      EN: Iniquity has risen as a rod of wickedness: nothing from them, nothing from the people, nor from their clamor; and there will be no rest among them.

7:12  LA: Venit tempus, appropinquavit dies : qui emit, non lætetur, et qui vendit, non lugeat : quia ira super omnem populum ejus.
      EN: The time has come; the day has drawn near. Let the buyer not rejoice, and the seller not mourn, for wrath is upon all its people.

7:13  LA: Quia qui vendit, ad id quod vendidit non revertetur : et adhuc in viventibus vita eorum : visio enim ad omnem multitudinem ejus non regredietur, et vir in iniquitate vitæ suæ non confortabitur.]
      EN: For the seller will not return to what he sold, though their life is still among the living. For the vision against its whole multitude will not turn back, and a man will not be strengthened in the iniquity of his life.

7:14  LA: [Canite tuba, præparentur omnes : et non est qui vadat ad prælium : ira enim mea super universum populum ejus.
      EN: Blow the trumpet; let everyone make ready! Yet there is no one to go to battle, for My wrath is upon all its people.

7:15  LA: Gladium foris, et pestis et fames intrinsecus : qui in agro est, gladio morietur, et qui in civitate, pestilentia et fame devorabuntur.
      EN: The sword is outside, and pestilence and famine are within. Whoever is in the field will die by the sword, and those in the city will be devoured by pestilence and famine.

7:16  LA: Et salvabuntur qui fugerint ex eis : et erunt in montibus quasi columbæ convallium omnes trepidi, unusquisque in iniquitate sua.
      EN: Those of them who flee will be saved, and they will be on the mountains like doves of the valleys, all trembling, each in his iniquity.

7:17  LA: Omnes manus dissolventur, et omnia genua fluent aquis.
      EN: All hands will fall limp, and all knees will flow with water.

7:18  LA: Et accingent se ciliciis, et operiet eos formido : et in omni facie confusio, et in universis capitibus eorum calvitium.
      EN: They will gird themselves with sackcloth, and terror will cover them. Shame will be on every face and baldness on all their heads.

7:19  LA: Argentum eorum foras projicietur, et aurum eorum in sterquilinium erit : argentum eorum et aurum eorum non valebit liberare eos in die furoris Domini : animam suam non saturabunt, et ventres eorum non implebuntur, quia scandalum iniquitatis eorum factum est.
      EN: Their silver will be thrown outside, and their gold will become a dung heap. Their silver and their gold will be unable to deliver them on the day of the Lord’s fury. They will not satisfy their souls, and their stomachs will not be filled, because it has become the stumbling block of their iniquity.

7:20  LA: Et ornamentum monilium suorum in superbiam posuerunt, et imagines abominationum suarum et simulacrorum fecerunt ex eo : propter hoc dedi eis illud in immunditiam.
      EN: They made the beauty of their jewelry a source of pride, and from it they made images of their abominations and idols. For this reason I have made it an unclean thing for them.

7:21  LA: Et dabo illud in manus alienorum ad diripiendum, et impiis terræ in prædam, et contaminabunt illud.
      EN: I will give it into the hands of strangers to plunder, and to the wicked of the earth as spoil; and they will defile it.

7:22  LA: Et avertam faciem meam ab eis, et violabunt arcanum meum : et introibunt in illud emissarii, et contaminabunt illud.
      EN: I will turn My face away from them, and they will violate My secret place. Intruders will enter it and defile it.

7:23  LA: Fac conclusionem, quoniam terra plena est judicio sanguinum, et civitas plena iniquitate.
      EN: Make an enclosure, for the land is full of judgments of bloodshed, and the city is full of iniquity.

7:24  LA: Et adducam pessimos de gentibus, et possidebunt domos eorum : et quiescere faciam superbiam potentium, et possidebunt sanctuaria eorum.
      EN: I will bring the worst of the nations, and they will possess their houses. I will put an end to the pride of the mighty, and they will possess their sanctuaries.

7:25  LA: Angustia superveniente, requirent pacem, et non erit.
      EN: When distress comes upon them, they will seek peace, and there will be none.

7:26  LA: Conturbatio super conturbationem veniet, et auditus super auditum : et quærent visionem de propheta, et lex peribit a sacerdote, et consilium a senioribus.
      EN: Disaster upon disaster will come, and report upon report. They will seek a vision from a prophet, but law will perish from the priest and counsel from the elders.

7:27  LA: Rex lugebit, et princeps induetur mœrore, et manus populi terræ conturbabuntur : secundum viam eorum faciam eis, et secundum judicia eorum judicabo eos, et scient quia ego Dominus.]
      EN: The king will mourn, and the ruler will be clothed in grief, and the hands of the people of the land will be shaken. I will deal with them according to their way, and judge them according to their judgments; and they will know that I am the Lord.”

EZEKIEL 8

8:1  LA: Et factum est in anno sexto, in sexto mense, in quinta mensis, ego sedebam in domo mea, et senes Juda sedebant coram me, et cecidit ibi super me manus Domini Dei.
     EN: In the sixth year, in the sixth month, on the fifth day of the month, I was sitting in my house, and the elders of Judah were sitting before me; and there the hand of the Lord God fell upon me.

8:2  LA: Et vidi : et ecce similitudo quasi aspectus ignis : ab aspectu lumborum ejus et deorsum, ignis : et a lumbis ejus et sursum, quasi aspectus splendoris, ut visio electri.
     EN: I looked, and behold, a likeness like the appearance of fire: from the appearance of His waist downward, fire; and from His waist upward, something like the appearance of brightness, like the sight of electrum.

8:3  LA: Et emissa similitudo manus apprehendit me in cincinno capitis mei, et elevavit me spiritus inter terram et cælum : et adduxit me in Jerusalem, in visione Dei, juxta ostium interius quod respiciebat ad aquilonem, ubi erat statutum idolum zeli ad provocandam æmulationem.
     EN: The likeness of a hand was stretched out and seized me by a lock of hair on my head; and a spirit lifted me between earth and heaven and brought me to Jerusalem, in a vision of God, beside the inner gate facing north, where the idol of jealousy had been set up to provoke jealousy.

8:4  LA: Et ecce ibi gloria Dei Israël, secundum visionem quam videram in campo.
     EN: And behold, the glory of the God of Israel was there, like the vision I had seen in the plain.

8:5  LA: Et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, leva oculos tuos ad viam aquilonis. Et levavi oculos meos ad viam aquilonis, et ecce ab aquilone portæ altaris idolum zeli in ipso introitu.
     EN: He said to me, “Son of man, lift your eyes toward the north.” I lifted my eyes toward the north, and behold, north of the altar gate, this idol of jealousy was at the very entrance.

8:6  LA: Et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, putasne vides tu quid isti faciunt, abominationes magnas quas domus Israël facit hic, ut procul recedam a sanctuario meo ? et adhuc conversus videbis abominationes majores.
     EN: He said to me, “Son of man, do you see what these people are doing, the great abominations that the house of Israel commits here, so that I must withdraw far from My sanctuary? Turn again, and you will see still greater abominations.”

8:7  LA: Et introduxit me ad ostium atrii, et vidi, et ecce foramen unum in pariete.
     EN: He brought me to the entrance of the court; and I looked, and behold, there was a hole in the wall.

8:8  LA: Et dixit ad me : Fili hominis, fode parietem. Et cum fodissem parietem, apparuit ostium unum.
     EN: He said to me, “Son of man, dig through the wall.” When I had dug through the wall, a doorway appeared.

8:9  LA: Et dixit ad me : Ingredere, et vide abominationes pessimas quas isti faciunt hic.
     EN: He said to me, “Go in and see the utterly evil abominations these people are committing here.”

8:10  LA: Et ingressus vidi, et ecce omnis similitudo reptilium et animalium, abominatio, et universa idola domus Israël, depicta erant in pariete in circuitu per totum :
      EN: I went in and looked, and behold, every likeness of creeping things and animals—an abomination—and all the idols of the house of Israel were painted on the wall, all around it.

8:11  LA: et septuaginta viri de senioribus domus Israël : et Jezonias filius Saphan stabat in medio eorum stantium ante picturas : et unusquisque habebat thuribulum in manu sua, et vapor nebulæ de thure consurgebat.
      EN: Seventy men of the elders of the house of Israel were there, and Jaazaniah son of Shaphan stood among them as they stood before the pictures. Each had a censer in his hand, and the vapor of a cloud of incense was rising.

8:12  LA: Et dixit ad me : Certe vides, fili hominis, quæ seniores domus Israël faciunt in tenebris, unusquisque in abscondito cubiculi sui : dicunt enim : Non videt Dominus nos ; dereliquit Dominus terram.
      EN: He said to me, “Surely you see, son of man, what the elders of the house of Israel are doing in the darkness, each in the concealment of his chamber. For they say, ‘The Lord does not see us; the Lord has abandoned the land.’”

8:13  LA: Et dixit ad me : Adhuc conversus videbis abominationes majores, quas isti faciunt.
      EN: He said to me, “Turn again, and you will see still greater abominations that these people commit.”

8:14  LA: Et introduxit me per ostium portæ domus Domini quod respiciebat ad aquilonem, et ecce ibi mulieres sedebant plangentes Adonidem.
      EN: He brought me through the entrance of the gate of the Lord’s house facing north, and behold, women were sitting there mourning Adonis.

8:15  LA: Et dixit ad me : Certe vidisti, fili hominis : adhuc conversus videbis abominationes majores his.
      EN: He said to me, “Surely you have seen, son of man. Turn again, and you will see abominations greater than these.”

8:16  LA: Et introduxit me in atrium domus Domini interius, et ecce in ostio templi Domini, inter vestibulum et altare, quasi viginti quinque viri dorsa habentes contra templum Domini, et facies ad orientem : et adorabant ad ortum solis.
      EN: He brought me into the inner court of the Lord’s house, and behold, at the entrance of the Lord’s temple, between the porch and the altar, there were about twenty-five men with their backs to the Lord’s temple and their faces toward the east; and they were worshiping toward the rising sun.

8:17  LA: Et dixit ad me : Certe vidisti, fili hominis : numquid leve est hoc domui Juda, ut facerent abominationes istas quas fecerunt hic, quia replentes terram iniquitate, conversi sunt ad irritandum me ? et ecce applicant ramum ad nares suas.
      EN: He said to me, “Surely you have seen, son of man. Is it a small thing for the house of Judah to commit these abominations that they have committed here? For after filling the land with iniquity, they have turned to provoke Me; and behold, they put a branch to their noses.

8:18  LA: Ergo et ego faciam in furore : non parcet oculus meus, nec miserebor : et cum clamaverint ad aures meas voce magna, non exaudiam eos.
      EN: Therefore I too will act in fury. My eye will not spare, and I will not have mercy; and when they cry into My ears with a loud voice, I will not hear them.”

EZEKIEL 9

9:1  LA: Et clamavit in auribus meis voce magna, dicens : Appropinquaverunt visitationes urbis, et unusquisque vas interfectionis habet in manu sua.
     EN: He cried into my ears with a loud voice, saying, “The visitations of the city have drawn near, and each man has an instrument of slaughter in his hand.”

9:2  LA: Et ecce sex viri veniebant de via portæ superioris, quæ respicit ad aquilonem, et uniuscujusque vas interitus in manu ejus : vir quoque unus in medio eorum vestitus erat lineis, et atramentarium scriptoris ad renes ejus : et ingressi sunt, et steterunt juxta altare æreum.
     EN: And behold, six men were coming from the direction of the upper gate that faces north, each with an instrument of destruction in his hand. One man among them was clothed in linen, with a writer’s inkhorn at his waist. They came in and stood beside the bronze altar.

9:3  LA: Et gloria Domini Israël assumpta est de cherub, quæ erat super eum ad limen domus : et vocavit virum qui indutus erat lineis, et atramentarium scriptoris habebat in lumbis suis :
     EN: The glory of the Lord of Israel was lifted from the cherub above which it rested, to the threshold of the house. He called the man who was clothed in linen and had a writer’s inkhorn at his waist.

9:4  LA: et dixit Dominus ad eum : Transi per mediam civitatem, in medio Jerusalem, et signa thau super frontes virorum gementium et dolentium super cunctis abominationibus quæ fiunt in medio ejus.
     EN: The Lord said to him, “Pass through the midst of the city, through the midst of Jerusalem, and mark a tau on the foreheads of the men who groan and grieve over all the abominations committed within it.”

9:5  LA: Et illis dixit, audiente me : Transite per civitatem sequentes eum, et percutite : non parcat oculus vester, neque misereamini :
     EN: To the others He said in my hearing, “Pass through the city after him and strike. Let your eye not spare, and have no mercy.

9:6  LA: senem, adolescentulum et virginem, parvulum et mulieres interficite usque ad internecionem : omnem autem super quem videritis thau, ne occidatis : et a sanctuario meo incipite. Cœperunt ergo a viris senioribus, qui erant ante faciem domus.
     EN: Kill old men, young men and maidens, little children and women, to utter destruction. But do not kill anyone on whom you see the tau; and begin at My sanctuary.” So they began with the elders who were before the house.

9:7  LA: Et dixit ad eos : Contaminate domum, et implete atria interfectis ; egredimini. Et egressi sunt, et percutiebant eos qui erant in civitate.
     EN: He said to them, “Defile the house, and fill the courts with the slain. Go out.” They went out and struck down those who were in the city.

9:8  LA: Et cæde completa, remansi ego, ruique super faciem meam, et clamans aio : Heu ! heu ! heu ! Domine Deus : ergone disperdes omnes reliquias Israël, effundens furorem tuum super Jerusalem ?
     EN: When the slaughter was completed, I remained. I fell on my face and cried out, saying, “Alas, alas, alas, Lord God! Will You then destroy the entire remnant of Israel by pouring out Your fury upon Jerusalem?”

9:9  LA: Et dixit ad me : Iniquitas domus Israël et Juda magna est nimis valde, et repleta est terra sanguinibus, et civitas repleta est aversione : dixerunt enim : Dereliquit Dominus terram, et Dominus non videt.
     EN: He said to me, “The iniquity of the house of Israel and Judah is exceedingly great. The land is filled with bloodshed, and the city is filled with apostasy. For they have said, ‘The Lord has abandoned the land, and the Lord does not see.’

9:10  LA: Igitur et meus non parcet oculus, neque miserebor : viam eorum super caput eorum reddam.
      EN: Therefore My eye too will not spare, and I will not have mercy. I will bring their way back upon their own heads.”

9:11  LA: Et ecce vir qui erat indutus lineis, qui habebat atramentarium in dorso suo, respondit verbum, dicens : Feci sicut præcepisti mihi.
      EN: And behold, the man clothed in linen, who had the inkhorn at his back, gave his report, saying, “I have done as You commanded me.”

EZEKIEL 10

10:1  LA: Et vidi : et ecce in firmamento quod erat super caput cherubim, quasi lapis sapphirus, quasi species similitudinis solii, apparuit super ea.
      EN: I looked, and behold, in the firmament above the heads of the cherubim, something like sapphire, like the appearance of the likeness of a throne, appeared over them.

10:2  LA: Et dixit ad virum qui indutus erat lineis, et ait : Ingredere in medio rotarum quæ sunt subtus cherubim, et imple manum tuam prunis ignis quæ sunt inter cherubim, et effunde super civitatem. Ingressusque est in conspectu meo.
      EN: He spoke to the man clothed in linen and said, “Go in among the wheels beneath the cherubim. Fill your hand with burning coals from between the cherubim, and scatter them over the city.” And he went in before my eyes.

10:3  LA: Cherubim autem stabant a dextris domus cum ingrederetur vir, et nubes implevit atrium interius.
      EN: The cherubim were standing to the right of the house when the man went in, and the cloud filled the inner court.

10:4  LA: Et elevata est gloria Domini desuper cherub ad limen domus : et repleta est domus nube, et atrium repletum est splendore gloriæ Domini.
      EN: The glory of the Lord rose from above the cherub to the threshold of the house. The house was filled with the cloud, and the court was filled with the brightness of the Lord’s glory.

10:5  LA: Et sonitus alarum cherubim audiebatur usque ad atrium exterius, quasi vox Dei omnipotentis loquentis.
      EN: The sound of the cherubim’s wings could be heard as far as the outer court, like the voice of Almighty God speaking.

10:6  LA: Cumque præcepisset viro qui indutus erat lineis, dicens : Sume ignem de medio rotarum quæ sunt inter cherubim : ingressus ille stetit juxta rotam.
      EN: When He commanded the man clothed in linen, saying, “Take fire from among the wheels between the cherubim,” the man went in and stood beside a wheel.

10:7  LA: Et extendit cherub manum de medio cherubim ad ignem qui erat inter cherubim, et sumpsit, et dedit in manus ejus qui indutus erat lineis : qui accipiens egressus est.
      EN: The cherub stretched out a hand from among the cherubim toward the fire between the cherubim, took some, and placed it in the hands of the man clothed in linen. He received it and went out.

10:8  LA: Et apparuit in cherubim similitudo manus hominis subtus pennas eorum.
      EN: In the cherubim there appeared the likeness of a human hand beneath their wings.

10:9  LA: Et vidi : et ecce quatuor rotæ juxta cherubim : rota una juxta cherub unum, et rota alia juxta cherub unum : species autem rotarum erat quasi visio lapidis chrysolithi :
      EN: I looked, and behold, four wheels were beside the cherubim: one wheel beside one cherub, and another wheel beside another cherub. The appearance of the wheels was like the sight of a chrysolite stone.

10:10  LA: et aspectus earum similitudo una quatuor, quasi sit rota in medio rotæ.
       EN: As for their appearance, all four had one likeness, as though a wheel were within a wheel.

10:11  LA: Cumque ambularent, in quatuor partes gradiebantur, et non revertebantur ambulantes : sed ad locum ad quem ire declinabat quæ prima erat, sequebantur et ceteræ, nec convertebantur.
       EN: When they moved, they advanced in four directions and did not turn back as they moved. Wherever the first one turned to go, the others also followed, without turning around.

10:12  LA: Et omne corpus earum, et colla, et manus, et pennæ, et circuli, plena erant oculis in circuitu quatuor rotarum.
       EN: Their whole body, their necks, hands, wings, and rims were full of eyes, all around the four wheels.

10:13  LA: Et rotas istas vocavit volubiles, audiente me.
       EN: In my hearing He called these wheels “the revolving ones.”

10:15  LA: Et elevata sunt cherubim : ipsum est animal quod videram juxta fluvium Chobar.
       EN: The cherubim rose up. This was the living creature that I had seen beside the river Chebar.

10:16  LA: Cumque ambularent cherubim, ibant pariter et rotæ juxta ea : et cum elevarent cherubim alas suas ut exaltarentur de terra, non residebant rotæ, sed et ipsæ juxta erant.
       EN: When the cherubim moved, the wheels moved together beside them; and when the cherubim lifted their wings to rise from the ground, the wheels did not remain behind, but were also beside them.

10:17  LA: Stantibus illis stabant, et cum elevatis elevabantur : spiritus enim vitæ erat in eis.
       EN: When those stood, these stood; and when those rose, these rose, for the spirit of life was in them.

10:18  LA: Et egressa est gloria Domini a limine templi, et stetit super cherubim.
       EN: The glory of the Lord went out from the threshold of the temple and stood above the cherubim.

10:19  LA: Et elevantia cherubim alas suas, exaltata sunt a terra coram me : et illis egredientibus, rotæ quoque subsecutæ sunt : et stetit in introitu portæ domus Domini orientalis, et gloria Dei Israël erat super ea.
       EN: The cherubim lifted their wings and rose from the ground before me. As they went out, the wheels also followed; and it stood at the entrance of the eastern gate of the Lord’s house, and the glory of the God of Israel was above them.

10:20  LA: Ipsum est animal quod vidi subter Deum Israël juxta fluvium Chobar, et intellexi quia cherubim essent.
       EN: This was the living creature that I had seen beneath the God of Israel beside the river Chebar, and I understood that they were cherubim.

10:21  LA: Quatuor vultus uni, et quatuor alæ uni : et similitudo manus hominis sub alis eorum.
       EN: Each had four faces, and each had four wings, and the likeness of a human hand was beneath their wings.

10:22  LA: Et similitudo vultuum eorum, ipsi vultus quos videram juxta fluvium Chobar, et intuitus eorum, et impetus singulorum ante faciem suam ingredi.
       EN: As for the likeness of their faces, they were the very faces I had seen beside the river Chebar, with the same appearance; and the impulse of each was to go straight ahead.

EZEKIEL 11

11:1  LA: Et elevavit me spiritus, et introduxit me ad portam domus Domini orientalem, quæ respicit ad solis ortum : et ecce in introitu portæ viginti quinque viri : et vidi in medio eorum Jezoniam filium Azur, et Pheltiam filium Banaiæ, principes populi.
      EN: A spirit lifted me and brought me to the eastern gate of the Lord’s house, which faces the rising sun. And behold, at the entrance of the gate there were twenty-five men. Among them I saw Jaazaniah son of Azzur and Pelatiah son of Benaiah, rulers of the people.

11:2  LA: Dixitque ad me : Fili hominis, hi sunt viri qui cogitant iniquitatem, et tractant consilium pessimum in urbe ista,
      EN: He said to me, “Son of man, these are the men who devise iniquity and discuss utterly evil plans in this city,

11:3  LA: dicentes : Nonne dudum ædificatæ sunt domus ? hæc est lebes, nos autem carnes.
      EN: saying, ‘Were not the houses built long ago? This city is the cauldron, and we are the meat.’

11:4  LA: Idcirco vaticinare de eis, vaticinare, fili hominis.
      EN: Therefore prophesy about them; prophesy, son of man.”

11:5  LA: Et irruit in me spiritus Domini, et dixit ad me : Loquere : Hæc dicit Dominus : Sic locuti estis, domus Israël, et cogitationes cordis vestri ego novi.
      EN: The Spirit of the Lord rushed upon me, and He said to me, “Speak: Thus says the Lord: This is what you have said, house of Israel, and I know the thoughts of your heart.

11:6  LA: Plurimos occidistis in urbe hac, et implestis vias ejus interfectis.
      EN: You have killed very many in this city and filled its streets with the slain.

11:7  LA: Propterea hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Interfecti vestri, quos posuistis in medio ejus, hi sunt carnes, et hæc est lebes : et educam vos de medio ejus.
      EN: Therefore thus says the Lord God: Your slain, whom you have laid within it, are the meat, and this city is the cauldron; but I will bring you out of it.

11:8  LA: Gladium metuistis, et gladium inducam super vos, ait Dominus Deus.
      EN: You have feared the sword, and I will bring a sword upon you, says the Lord God.

11:9  LA: Et ejiciam vos de medio ejus, daboque vos in manu hostium, et faciam in vobis judicia.
      EN: I will cast you out of it and give you into the hands of enemies, and I will execute judgments upon you.

11:10  LA: Gladio cadetis : in finibus Israël judicabo vos, et scietis quia ego Dominus.
       EN: You will fall by the sword; at the borders of Israel I will judge you, and you will know that I am the Lord.

11:11  LA: Hæc non erit vobis in lebetem, et vos non eritis in medio ejus in carnes : in finibus Israël judicabo vos,
       EN: This city will not be a cauldron for you, nor will you be meat within it. At the borders of Israel I will judge you,

11:12  LA: et scietis quia ego Dominus : quia in præceptis meis non ambulastis, et judicia mea non fecistis, sed juxta judicia gentium quæ in circuitu vestro sunt estis operati.
       EN: and you will know that I am the Lord, because you have not walked in My commands or carried out My judgments, but have acted according to the judgments of the nations around you.”

11:13  LA: Et factum est cum prophetarem, Pheltias filius Banaiæ mortuus est : et cecidi in faciem meam clamans voce magna, et dixi : Heu ! heu ! heu ! Domine Deus, consummationem tu facis reliquiarum Israël ?
       EN: As I was prophesying, Pelatiah son of Benaiah died. I fell on my face and cried out with a loud voice, saying, “Alas, alas, alas, Lord God! Are You making an end of the remnant of Israel?”

11:14  LA: Et factum est verbum Domini ad me, dicens :
       EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

11:15  LA: Fili hominis, fratres tui, fratres tui, viri propinqui tui, et omnis domus Israël, universi quibus dixerunt habitatores Jerusalem : Longe recedite a Domino : nobis data est terra in possessionem.
       EN: “Son of man, your brothers, your brothers, your kinsmen, and the whole house of Israel—all of them are those to whom the inhabitants of Jerusalem have said, ‘Stay far away from the Lord; the land has been given to us as a possession.’

11:16  LA: Propterea hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Quia longe feci eos in gentibus, et quia dispersi eos in terris : ero eis in sanctificationem modicam in terris ad quas venerunt.
       EN: Therefore thus says the Lord God: Because I have sent them far away among the nations and because I have scattered them through the lands, I will be a little sanctuary for them in the lands to which they have come.

11:17  LA: Propterea loquere : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Congregabo vos de populis, et adunabo de terris in quibus dispersi estis, daboque vobis humum Israël.
       EN: Therefore say: Thus says the Lord God: I will gather you from the peoples and bring you together from the lands through which you have been scattered, and I will give you the soil of Israel.

11:18  LA: Et ingredientur illuc, et auferent omnes offensiones, cunctasque abominationes ejus de illa.
       EN: They will enter it and remove from it all its offensive things and all its abominations.

11:19  LA: Et dabo eis cor unum, et spiritum novum tribuam in visceribus eorum : et auferam cor lapideum de carne eorum, et dabo eis cor carneum,
       EN: I will give them one heart and put a new spirit within them. I will take the stony heart out of their flesh and give them a heart of flesh,

11:20  LA: ut in præceptis meis ambulent, et judicia mea custodiant, faciantque ea, et sint mihi in populum, et ego sim eis in Deum.
       EN: so that they may walk in My commands, keep My judgments and carry them out, and be My people, and I may be their God.

11:21  LA: Quorum cor post offendicula et abominationes suas ambulat, horum viam in capite suo ponam, dicit Dominus Deus.
       EN: But as for those whose heart follows their offensive things and abominations, I will bring their way upon their own heads, says the Lord God.”

11:22  LA: Et elevaverunt cherubim alas suas, et rotæ cum eis, et gloria Dei Israël erat super ea :
       EN: The cherubim lifted their wings, and the wheels were with them, and the glory of the God of Israel was above them.

11:23  LA: et ascendit gloria Domini de medio civitatis, stetitque super montem qui est ad orientem urbis.
       EN: The glory of the Lord rose from the midst of the city and stood upon the mountain east of the city.

11:24  LA: Et spiritus levavit me, adduxitque in Chaldæam ad transmigrationem, in visione, in spiritu Dei : et sublata est a me visio quam videram.
       EN: A spirit lifted me and brought me to the exiles in Chaldea, in a vision, in the Spirit of God. Then the vision I had seen was taken up from me.

11:25  LA: Et locutus sum ad transmigrationem omnia verba Domini quæ ostenderat mihi.
       EN: I spoke to the exiles all the words of the Lord that He had shown me.

EZEKIEL 12

12:1  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini ad me, dicens :
      EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

12:2  LA: Fili hominis, in medio domus exasperantis tu habitas : qui oculos habent ad videndum, et non vident, et aures ad audiendum, et non audiunt : quia domus exasperans est.
      EN: “Son of man, you dwell in the midst of an exasperating house. They have eyes to see, yet do not see, and ears to hear, yet do not hear, because they are an exasperating house.

12:3  LA: Tu ergo, fili hominis, fac tibi vasa transmigrationis, et transmigrabis per diem coram eis. Transmigrabis autem de loco tuo ad locum alterum in conspectu eorum, si forte aspiciant, quia domus exasperans est :
      EN: Therefore, son of man, prepare the belongings of an exile for yourself, and go into exile by day before them. Move from your place to another place in their sight, in case they may see, for they are an exasperating house.

12:4  LA: et efferes foras vasa tua quasi vasa transmigrantis per diem in conspectu eorum : tu autem egredieris vespere coram eis, sicut egreditur migrans.
      EN: Bring your belongings outside like the belongings of an exile, by day in their sight. But you shall go out in the evening before them, as an exile goes out.

12:5  LA: Ante oculos eorum perfode tibi parietem, et egredieris per eum.
      EN: Before their eyes, dig through the wall for yourself, and go out through it.

12:6  LA: In conspectu eorum in humeris portaberis ; in caligine effereris : faciem tuam velabis, et non videbis terram, quia portentum dedi te domui Israël.
      EN: In their sight you shall be carried on shoulders; in the darkness you shall be borne out. Cover your face so that you do not see the land, for I have made you a portent for the house of Israel.”

12:7  LA: Feci ergo sicut præceperat mihi Dominus : vasa mea protuli quasi vasa transmigrantis per diem, et vespere perfodi mihi parietem manu : et in caligine egressus sum, in humeris portatus in conspectu eorum.
      EN: So I did as the Lord had commanded me. I brought out my belongings like the belongings of an exile by day, and in the evening I dug through the wall for myself by hand. In the darkness I went out, carried on shoulders in their sight.

12:8  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini mane ad me, dicens :
      EN: In the morning the word of the Lord came to me, saying,

12:9  LA: Fili hominis, numquid non dixerunt ad te domus Israël, domus exasperans : Quid tu facis ?
      EN: “Son of man, has not the house of Israel, that exasperating house, said to you, ‘What are you doing?’

12:10  LA: Dic ad eos : [Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Super ducem onus istud, qui est in Jerusalem, et super omnem domum Israël, quæ est in medio eorum.]
       EN: Say to them: Thus says the Lord God: This burden is upon the ruler who is in Jerusalem, and upon the whole house of Israel in their midst.

12:11  LA: Dic : [Ego portentum vestrum : quomodo feci, sic fiet illis : in transmigrationem et in captivitatem ibunt.
       EN: Say: I am your portent. As I have done, so it will be done to them. They will go into exile and captivity.

12:12  LA: Et dux qui est in medio eorum, in humeris portabitur ; in caligine egredietur : parietem perfodient, ut educant eum ; facies ejus operietur, ut non videat oculo terram.
       EN: The ruler in their midst will be carried on shoulders; he will go out in the darkness. They will dig through the wall to bring him out, and his face will be covered so that his eye does not see the land.

12:13  LA: Et extendam rete meum super eum, et capietur in sagena mea : et adducam eum in Babylonem, in terram Chaldæorum, et ipsam non videbit : ibique morietur.
       EN: I will spread My net over him, and he will be caught in My dragnet. I will bring him to Babylon, to the land of the Chaldeans, yet he will not see it; and there he will die.

12:14  LA: Et omnes qui circa eum sunt, præsidium ejus, et agmina ejus, dispergam in omnem ventum, et gladium evaginabo post eos.
       EN: All those around him, his guard and his troops, I will scatter to every wind, and I will unsheathe a sword after them.

12:15  LA: Et scient quia ego Dominus, quando dispersero illos in gentibus, et disseminavero eos in terris.
       EN: They will know that I am the Lord when I scatter them among the nations and disperse them through the lands.

12:16  LA: Et relinquam ex eis viros paucos a gladio, et fame, et pestilentia, ut enarrent omnia scelera eorum in gentibus ad quas ingredientur, et scient quia ego Dominus.]
       EN: I will spare a few men of them from sword, famine, and pestilence, so that they may recount all their crimes among the nations to which they go; and they will know that I am the Lord.”

12:17  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini ad me, dicens :
       EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

12:18  LA: [Fili hominis, panem tuum in conturbatione comede, sed et aquam tuam in festinatione et mœrore bibe.
       EN: “Son of man, eat your bread in turmoil, and drink your water in haste and grief.

12:19  LA: Et dices ad populum terræ : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus ad eos qui habitant in Jerusalem, in terra Israël : Panem suum in sollicitudine comedent, et aquam suam in desolatione bibent : ut desoletur terra a multitudine sua, propter iniquitatem omnium qui habitant in ea.
       EN: Say to the people of the land: Thus says the Lord God to those who dwell in Jerusalem, in the land of Israel: They will eat their bread in anxiety and drink their water in desolation, so that the land may be stripped of its abundance because of the iniquity of all who dwell in it.

12:20  LA: Et civitates quæ nunc habitantur, desolatæ erunt, terraque deserta, et scietis quia ego Dominus.]
       EN: The cities now inhabited will be desolate, and the land deserted; and you will know that I am the Lord.”

12:21  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini ad me, dicens :
       EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

12:22  LA: [Fili hominis, quod est proverbium istud vobis in terra Israël, dicentium : In longum differentur dies, et peribit omnis visio ?
       EN: “Son of man, what is this proverb you have in the land of Israel, saying, ‘The days will be delayed a long time, and every vision will perish’?

12:23  LA: Ideo dic ad eos : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Quiescere faciam proverbium istud, neque vulgo dicetur ultra in Israël : et loquere ad eos quod appropinquaverint dies, et sermo omnis visionis.
       EN: Therefore say to them: Thus says the Lord God: I will put an end to this proverb, and it will no longer be commonly spoken in Israel. Tell them that the days have drawn near, and the word of every vision.

12:24  LA: Non enim erit ultra omnis visio cassa, neque divinatio ambigua in medio filiorum Israël :
       EN: For there will no longer be any empty vision or ambiguous divination among the children of Israel.

12:25  LA: quia ego Dominus loquar, et quodcumque locutus fuero verbum, fiet, et non prolongabitur amplius : sed in diebus vestris, domus exasperans, loquar verbum, et faciam illud, dicit Dominus Deus.]
       EN: For I, the Lord, will speak, and whatever word I speak will come to pass and will no longer be delayed. In your days, exasperating house, I will speak a word and carry it out, says the Lord God.”

12:26  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini ad me, dicens :
       EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

12:27  LA: [Fili hominis, ecce domus Israël dicentium : Visio quam hic videt, in dies multos et in tempora longa iste prophetat.
       EN: “Son of man, behold, the house of Israel is saying, ‘The vision this man sees is for many days ahead, and he prophesies of distant times.’

12:28  LA: Propterea dic ad eos : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Non prolongabitur ultra omnis sermo meus : verbum quod locutus fuero, complebitur, dicit Dominus Deus.]
       EN: Therefore say to them: Thus says the Lord God: No word of Mine will be delayed any longer. The word I speak will be fulfilled, says the Lord God.”

EZEKIEL 13

13:1  LA: Et factus est sermo Domini ad me, dicens :
      EN: The word of the Lord came to me, saying,

13:2  LA: Fili hominis, vaticinare ad prophetas Israël qui prophetant, et dices prophetantibus de corde suo : [Audite verbum Domini.
      EN: “Son of man, prophesy to the prophets of Israel who are prophesying, and say to those who prophesy from their own hearts: Hear the word of the Lord.

13:3  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Væ prophetis insipientibus, qui sequuntur spiritum suum, et nihil vident !
      EN: Thus says the Lord God: Woe to the foolish prophets who follow their own spirit and see nothing!

13:4  LA: Quasi vulpes in desertis prophetæ tui, Israël, erant.
      EN: Your prophets, Israel, have been like foxes in the wastelands.

13:5  LA: Non ascendistis ex adverso, neque opposuistis murum pro domo Israël, ut staretis in prælio in die Domini.
      EN: You have not gone up to face the enemy or set up a wall for the house of Israel, so that you might stand in battle on the day of the Lord.

13:6  LA: Vident vana, et divinant mendacium, dicentes : Ait Dominus, cum Dominus non miserit eos : et perseveraverunt confirmare sermonem.
      EN: They see empty things and divine a lie, saying, ‘The Lord says,’ although the Lord has not sent them; and they persist in confirming their word.

13:7  LA: Numquid non visionem cassam vidistis, et divinationem mendacem locuti estis, et dicitis : Ait Dominus, cum ego non sim locutus ?
      EN: Have you not seen an empty vision and spoken a lying divination? Yet you say, ‘The Lord says,’ when I have not spoken.

13:8  LA: Propterea hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Quia locuti estis vana, et vidistis mendacium, ideo ecce ego ad vos, dicit Dominus Deus.
      EN: Therefore thus says the Lord God: Because you have spoken empty things and seen a lie, therefore behold, I am against you, says the Lord God.

13:9  LA: Et erit manus mea super prophetas qui vident vana, et divinant mendacium : in consilio populi mei non erunt, et in scriptura domus Israël non scribentur, nec in terram Israël ingredientur, et scietis quia ego Dominus Deus :
      EN: My hand will be against the prophets who see empty things and divine a lie. They will not be in the council of My people, nor be written in the register of the house of Israel, nor enter the land of Israel; and you will know that I am the Lord God.

13:10  LA: eo quod deceperint populum meum, dicentes : Pax, et non est pax : et ipse ædificabat parietem, illi autem liniebant eum luto absque paleis.
       EN: For they have deceived My people, saying, ‘Peace,’ when there is no peace. The people were building a wall, and these men were coating it with mud without straw.

13:11  LA: Dic ad eos qui liniunt absque temperatura, quod casurus sit : erit enim imber inundans, et dabo lapides prægrandes desuper irruentes, et ventum procellæ dissipantem.
       EN: Tell those who coat it with unmixed mortar that it will fall. For there will be a flooding rain, and I will send enormous stones crashing down from above and a storm wind to scatter it.

13:12  LA: Siquidem ecce cecidit paries : numquid non dicetur vobis : Ubi est litura quam linistis ?
       EN: Indeed, behold, the wall has fallen. Will they not say to you, ‘Where is the coating with which you coated it?’

13:13  LA: Propterea hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Et erumpere faciam spiritum tempestatum in indignatione mea, et imber inundans in furore meo erit, et lapides grandes in ira in consumptionem.
       EN: Therefore thus says the Lord God: I will make a tempestuous wind burst forth in My indignation, and there will be a flooding rain in My fury, and great stones in wrath, to bring destruction.

13:14  LA: Et destruam parietem quem linistis absque temperamento, et adæquabo eum terræ, et revelabitur fundamentum ejus : et cadet, et consumetur in medio ejus, et scietis quia ego sum Dominus.
       EN: I will tear down the wall that you coated with unmixed mortar and level it to the ground, and its foundation will be laid bare. It will fall and be consumed in its midst; and you will know that I am the Lord.

13:15  LA: Et complebo indignationem meam in pariete, et in his qui liniunt eum absque temperamento : dicamque vobis : Non est paries, et non sunt qui liniunt eum :
       EN: I will spend My indignation upon the wall and upon those who coat it with unmixed mortar. I will say to you, ‘The wall is no more, and those who coat it are no more’—

13:16  LA: prophetæ Israël, qui prophetant ad Jerusalem, et vident ei visionem pacis, et non est pax, ait Dominus Deus.]
       EN: the prophets of Israel who prophesy to Jerusalem and see a vision of peace for her, when there is no peace, says the Lord God.

13:17  LA: Et tu, fili hominis, pone faciem tuam contra filias populi tui quæ prophetant de corde suo : et vaticinare super eas,
       EN: “And you, son of man, set your face against the daughters of your people who prophesy from their own hearts, and prophesy against them.

13:18  LA: et dic : [Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Væ quæ consuunt pulvillos sub omni cubito manus, et faciunt cervicalia sub capite universæ ætatis ad capiendas animas : et cum caperent animas populi mei, vivificabant animas eorum !
       EN: Say: Thus says the Lord God: Woe to those who sew little cushions under every elbow and make pillows under the heads of people of every age to capture souls! While they captured the souls of My people, they kept their souls alive.

13:19  LA: Et violabant me ad populum meum propter pugillum hordei, et fragmen panis, ut interficerent animas quæ non moriuntur, et vivificarent animas quæ non vivunt, mentientes populo meo credenti mendaciis.
       EN: They profaned Me before My people for a handful of barley and a piece of bread, to kill souls that do not die and keep alive souls that do not live, lying to My people who believe lies.

13:20  LA: Propter hoc hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Ecce ego ad pulvillos vestros, quibus vos capitis animas volantes : et dirumpam eos de brachiis vestris, et dimittam animas quas vos capitis, animas ad volandum.
       EN: Therefore thus says the Lord God: Behold, I am against your little cushions, with which you capture flying souls. I will tear them from your arms and release the souls you capture—souls set free to fly.
